// FILE: ./{{"README"}}.md
# {{project-name}}

A production-ready Rust web application built on [Axum](https://github.com/tokio-rs/axum), following best practices from the [crates.io](https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io) backend implementation.

> Generated with [cargo-generate](https://github.com/cargo-generate/cargo-generate) from
> [axum-kickoff](https://github.com/luizvbo/axum-kickoff).

## Features

- **Modern Stack**: Axum 0.8 with Tokio async runtime
- **Database**: Toasty ORM with SQLite (zero-setup) or PostgreSQL
- **Authentication**: OAuth sign-in (GitHub, Google, Facebook, selectable at generation), session-based auth, and scoped API tokens
- **Frontend**: Server-side rendering with Askama, HTMX, and Alpine.js
- **Security**: Comprehensive middleware (security headers, rate limiting, CSRF, etc.)
- **Testing**: Integration test infrastructure with snapshot testing
- **Storage**: Local filesystem storage (pluggable architecture for future backends)
- **Background Jobs**: Built-in worker for async job processing
- **API Docs**: OpenAPI/Swagger UI out of the box (utoipa)
- **Optional subsystems** (selected at generation time): Prometheus metrics, Sentry error reporting, jemalloc allocator, Tailwind CSS

## Quick Start

### Prerequisites

- Rust (see `rust-toolchain.toml` for pinned version)
- [just](https://github.com/casey/just) (for running setup and other commands)
- Node.js and npm (for vendoring frontend dependencies)

### Installation

```bash
# Install dependencies and vendor JS libraries (HTMX, Alpine.js){% if tailwind %},
# then build the Tailwind stylesheet (rerun anytime with `just css`){% endif %}
just setup

# Copy environment variables
cp .env.sample .env

# Edit .env with your configuration
# Required: SESSION_KEY, WEB_ALLOWED_ORIGINS, and the client credentials for
# each OAuth provider you want to enable (e.g. GH_CLIENT_ID/GH_CLIENT_SECRET).
# For local development also keep APP_ENV=development (the default when unset
# is production, which enables Secure cookies, JSON logs, and disables /debug)

# Run the server
cargo run --bin {{project-name}} -- server
```

The server will start on `http://localhost:8888` by default.

### Configuration

Set the following environment variables in `.env`:

```bash
# Environment (defaults to production when unset)
APP_ENV=development

# Server
PORT=8888
DOMAIN_NAME=localhost

# Database
DATABASE_URL=sqlite:./{{crate_name}}.db

# Session
SESSION_KEY=your-secret-key-min-64-bytes

# OAuth providers: each is enabled when both its credentials are set.
# Which providers exist is chosen at generation time (oauth_* options).
{% if oauth_github %}# GitHub
GH_CLIENT_ID=your-github-client-id
GH_CLIENT_SECRET=your-github-client-secret
GH_REDIRECT_URI=http://localhost:8888/api/v1/auth/github/callback
{% endif %}{% if oauth_google %}# Google
GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID=your-google-client-id
GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET=your-google-client-secret
GOOGLE_REDIRECT_URI=http://localhost:8888/api/v1/auth/google/callback
{% endif %}{% if oauth_facebook %}# Facebook
FACEBOOK_CLIENT_ID=your-facebook-app-id
FACEBOOK_CLIENT_SECRET=your-facebook-app-secret
FACEBOOK_REDIRECT_URI=http://localhost:8888/api/v1/auth/facebook/callback
{% endif %}

# CORS
WEB_ALLOWED_ORIGINS=http://localhost:8888,http://127.0.0.1:8888

# Storage
STORAGE_PATH=./local_uploads
```

See [Configuration Documentation](docs/CONFIGURATION.md) for all available options.

## Using this repository as a template

This repository is a [cargo-generate](https://github.com/cargo-generate/cargo-generate) template:

```bash
cargo install cargo-generate
cargo generate --git https://github.com/luizvbo/axum-kickoff --name my-app
```

You'll be prompted for the options below (or pass `-d <key>=<value>` to skip prompts):

| Option | Values | Default | Effect |
| ------ | ------ | ------- | ------ |
| `project-name` | any crate-safe name | asked once | Package name, binary name, session cookie (`<crate_name>_session`), default SQLite file (`<crate_name>.db`), PostgreSQL `application_name`, API title, user-agent strings, and the on-page application branding |
| `database` | `sqlite` / `postgresql` | `sqlite` | `sqlite`: zero-setup file database. `postgresql`: adds the Toasty Postgres driver and a `postgresql://` default `DATABASE_URL`. SQLite stays enabled either way. The test suite uses in-memory SQLite regardless |
| `oauth_github` | `true` / `false` | `true` | Compiles in the GitHub OAuth provider, enabled at runtime when `GH_CLIENT_ID`/`GH_CLIENT_SECRET` are set |
| `oauth_google` | `true` / `false` | `false` | Same for Google (`GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`/`GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`) |
| `oauth_facebook` | `true` / `false` | `false` | Same for Facebook (`FACEBOOK_CLIENT_ID`/`FACEBOOK_CLIENT_SECRET`) |
| `metrics` | `true` / `false` | `false` | Adds the `prometheus` dependency, a `metrics` feature in `default`, the `/metrics` endpoint, and request instrumentation middleware |
| `sentry` | `true` / `false` | `false` | Adds `sentry` + `sentry-tracing` dependencies and feature, error-event capture via the tracing layer, and `SENTRY_DSN` support |
| `jemalloc` | `true` / `false` | `false` | Adds `tikv-jemallocator` and sets jemalloc as the global allocator |

The background worker, filesystem storage, and Swagger/OpenAPI subsystems are
unconditional parts of the application, not template options.

> **PostgreSQL note**: the template ships a migration set per backend
> (`migrations/` for SQLite, `migrations-postgres/` for PostgreSQL) and keeps
> only the one matching `database`, so `migrate` works out of the box on
> either backend. The test suite is unaffected: it runs on in-memory SQLite.

### Non-interactive generation

```bash
cargo generate --git https://github.com/luizvbo/axum-kickoff \
  --name my-app --silent \
  -d database=postgresql -d metrics=true -d sentry=true -d jemalloc=true
```

See the "Working on this template" section in
[docs/DEVELOPMENT.md](docs/DEVELOPMENT.md) for how the template itself is
developed and verified.

## Project Structure

```
{{project-name}}/
├── src/
│   ├── bin/           # Binary entry points
│   ├── controllers/   # HTTP request handlers
│   ├── middleware/    # Axum middleware
│   ├── models/        # Database models (Toasty)
│   ├── config/        # Configuration management
│   ├── util/          # Utility functions
│   ├── tests/         # Integration test infrastructure
│   └── ...
├── templates/         # Askama templates
├── static/           # Static assets
├── docs/             # Documentation
└── Cargo.toml        # Dependencies
```

## Development

### Running Tests

```bash
# Run all tests
cargo test

# Accept snapshot changes
cargo insta accept
```

### Database Migrations

```bash
# Apply pending migrations
cargo run --bin {{project-name}} -- migrate migration apply

# Generate a new migration after model changes
cargo run --bin {{project-name}} -- migrate migration generate
```

## Philosophy

{{project-name}} is designed with these principles:

1. **Simplicity First**: Single-crate architecture with clear module organization
2. **Zero-Setup Development**: SQLite and local filesystem for instant start
3. **Production-Ready Patterns**: Based on crates.io's battle-tested implementation
4. **Cost-Conscious**: Self-hostable with minimal external dependencies
5. **Gradual Complexity**: Start simple, upgrade features as needed
6. **Type Safety**: Leverage Rust's type system throughout

[advisories]
version = 2
db-path = "~/.cargo/advisory-db"
db-urls = ["https://github.com/rustsec/advisory-db"]
{% if database == "postgresql" %}# RUSTSEC-2025-0134: rustls-pemfile is unmaintained (repo archived Aug 2025),
# not vulnerable — 2.2.0 is a thin wrapper over rustls-pki-types, already in
# the tree. Reached only via toasty-driver-postgresql through the pinned
# toasty =0.11.0 (latest release); drop this once toasty migrates to
# rustls-pki-types' PemObject.
{% endif %}# RUSTSEC-2026-0374: basic-toml is unmaintained, not vulnerable — it parses
# askama.toml at compile time inside askama_derive. Even askama 0.16.1 still
# depends on it, so no upgrade path exists; drop this once askama moves off it.
ignore = [
    {% if database == "postgresql" %}"RUSTSEC-2025-0134",
    {% endif %}"RUSTSEC-2026-0374",
]

// FILE: ./README.md
# axum-kickoff

A [cargo-generate](https://github.com/cargo-generate/cargo-generate) template for production-ready Rust web applications built on [Axum](https://github.com/tokio-rs/axum), following best practices from the [crates.io](https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io) backend implementation.

This repository **is** the template. Its sources contain Liquid placeholders and do not compile as-is. Generating a project renders them into a fully working application.

## What you get

- **Modern Stack**: Axum 0.8 with Tokio async runtime
- **Database**: Toasty ORM with SQLite (zero-setup) or PostgreSQL
- **Authentication**: OAuth sign-in (GitHub, Google, Facebook, selectable at generation), session-based auth, and scoped API tokens
- **Frontend**: Server-side rendering with Askama, HTMX, and Alpine.js
- **Security**: Comprehensive middleware (security headers, rate limiting, CSRF, etc.)
- **Testing**: Integration test infrastructure with snapshot testing
- **Storage**: Local filesystem storage (pluggable architecture for future backends)
- **Background Jobs**: Built-in worker for async job processing
- **API Docs**: OpenAPI/Swagger UI out of the box (utoipa)
- **Optional subsystems** (selected at generation time): Prometheus metrics, Sentry error reporting, jemalloc allocator

## Generate a project

```bash
cargo install cargo-generate
cargo generate --git https://github.com/luizvbo/axum-kickoff --name my-app
```

You'll be prompted for the options below (or pass `-d <key>=<value>` to skip prompts):

| Option | Values | Default | Effect |
| ------ | ------ | ------- | ------ |
| `project-name` | any crate-safe name | asked once | Package name, binary name, session cookie (`<crate_name>_session`), default SQLite file (`<crate_name>.db`), PostgreSQL `application_name`, API title, user-agent strings, and the on-page application branding |
| `database` | `sqlite` / `postgresql` | `sqlite` | `sqlite`: zero-setup file database. `postgresql`: adds the Toasty Postgres driver and a `postgresql://` default `DATABASE_URL`. SQLite stays enabled either way. The test suite uses in-memory SQLite regardless |
| `oauth_github` | `true` / `false` | `true` | Compiles in the GitHub OAuth provider, enabled at runtime when `GH_CLIENT_ID`/`GH_CLIENT_SECRET` are set |
| `oauth_google` | `true` / `false` | `false` | Same for Google (`GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`/`GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`) |
| `oauth_facebook` | `true` / `false` | `false` | Same for Facebook (`FACEBOOK_CLIENT_ID`/`FACEBOOK_CLIENT_SECRET`) |
| `metrics` | `true` / `false` | `false` | Adds the `prometheus` dependency, a `metrics` feature in `default`, the `/metrics` endpoint, and request instrumentation middleware |
| `sentry` | `true` / `false` | `false` | Adds `sentry` + `sentry-tracing` dependencies and feature, error-event capture via the tracing layer, and `SENTRY_DSN` support |
| `jemalloc` | `true` / `false` | `false` | Adds `tikv-jemallocator` and sets jemalloc as the global allocator |
| `tailwind` | `true` / `false` | `false` | Adds `tailwindcss` + `@tailwindcss/cli` npm dev dependencies and a `just css` recipe (also run by `just setup`, `just run` and `just dev`) that compiles `static/css/input.css` into `static/css/style.css`, replacing the bundled hand-written stylesheet |

The background worker, filesystem storage, and Swagger/OpenAPI subsystems are
unconditional parts of the generated application, not options.

Non-interactive generation:

```bash
cargo generate --git https://github.com/luizvbo/axum-kickoff \
  --name my-app --silent \
  -d database=postgresql -d metrics=true -d sentry=true -d jemalloc=true
```

> **PostgreSQL note**: two migration sets are checked in — `migrations/`
> (SQLite dialect) and `migrations-postgres/` (PostgreSQL dialect).
> `database=postgresql` compiles in the Postgres driver, defaults
> `DATABASE_URL` to Postgres, and ships only the Postgres migration set, so
> `migrate` works against a real Postgres database out of the box. The test
> suite is unaffected: it runs on in-memory SQLite.

## After generation

```bash
cd my-app
just setup        # npm install + vendor JS libraries (HTMX, Alpine.js)
cp .env.sample .env  # then edit; required: SESSION_KEY, WEB_ALLOWED_ORIGINS,
                     # and credentials for each OAuth provider you enable
cargo run -- server  # http://localhost:8888
```

## Documentation

The generated project ships its own docs under `docs/`. Start with
[Getting Started](docs/GETTING_STARTED.md) and the
[Configuration reference](docs/CONFIGURATION.md). They are Liquid-rendered at
generation time and describe your project, not this repository.

## Working on this template

The checkout does not compile: `Cargo.toml` and several `src/` files contain
Liquid placeholders and conditionals. Verification happens on rendered output:
edit source → render → check the rendered project. `.github/workflows/template-ci.yml`
runs this over the option matrix and is the source of truth.

The README split: `README.md` (this file) is the repository's own readme and is
`ignore`d by cargo-generate. The readme shipped into generated projects lives in
`{{"README"}}.md`, and since cargo-generate renders Liquid in filenames too, it
lands as `README.md` in the output.

See [docs/TEMPLATE.md](docs/TEMPLATE.md) for the full authoring reference
(placeholders, tag-placement rules, conditional files) and the "Working on this
template" section of [docs/DEVELOPMENT.md](docs/DEVELOPMENT.md) for the
day-to-day workflow.

// FILE: ./justfile
# Justfile for {{project-name}}
#
# Install just: cargo install just
# Run commands: just <command>
# List all commands: just --list

# Default recipe - show available commands
default:
    @just --list

# ============================================================================
# Development Commands
# ============================================================================

[doc("Install dependencies and vendor JS libraries (run after clone or when package.json changes)")]
setup:
    @echo "📦 Installing dependencies..."
    npm install
    @echo "🚚 Vendoring JS libraries..."
    @mkdir -p static/vendor
    @cp node_modules/htmx.org/dist/htmx.min.js static/vendor/htmx.min.js
    @cp node_modules/@alpinejs/csp/dist/cdn.min.js static/vendor/alpine.js
{% if tailwind %}    @just css
{% endif %}
{% if tailwind %}[doc("Regenerate static/css/style.css with Tailwind CSS")]
css:
    npx @tailwindcss/cli -i static/css/input.css -o static/css/style.css --minify

{% endif %}[doc("Run the development server")]
run:{% if tailwind %} css{% endif %}
    cargo run --bin {{project-name}} -- server

[doc("Run the development server with auto-reload (requires cargo-watch)")]
dev:{% if tailwind %} css{% endif %}
    cargo watch -x 'run --bin {{project-name}} -- server'

[doc("Run tests with nextest faster test runner (requires cargo-nextest)")]
test:
    cargo nextest run

[doc("Run tests with nextest and accept updated snapshots")]
test-nextest-accept:
    # Accept all snapshot changes without prompting. Use this when you've
    # intentionally changed API responses and want to update the snapshots.
    cargo nextest run --accept

[doc("Run tests with nextest and review snapshot changes")]
test-nextest-review:
    # Review snapshot changes interactively. Use this to inspect what changed
    # before accepting. You'll be prompted to accept or reject each change.
    cargo nextest run --review

[doc("Run insta snapshot tests")]
snapshot:
    cargo insta test

[doc("Run insta snapshot tests and accept pending snapshots")]
snapshot-accept:
    cargo insta test --accept

[doc("Run tests with output")]
test-verbose:
    cargo test -- --nocapture

[doc("Run test coverage (add '--html' to generate an HTML report)")]
test-cov args='':
    @echo "🧪 Running test coverage (skipping test errors)..."
    APP_ENV=TEST cargo llvm-cov nextest --ignore-run-fail {% raw %}{{ if args != '' { args } else { "" } }}{% endraw %}

# ============================================================================
# Database Commands
# ============================================================================

[doc("Generate a new migration based on model changes")]
migration-generate:
    cargo run --bin {{project-name}} -- migrate migration generate

[doc("Apply pending migrations to the database")]
migration-apply:
    cargo run --bin {{project-name}} -- migrate migration apply

[doc("Create a schema snapshot for future migration generation")]
migration-snapshot:
    cargo run --bin {{project-name}} -- migrate migration snapshot

[doc("Drop the last migration file")]
migration-drop:
    cargo run --bin {{project-name}} -- migrate migration drop

[doc("Reset the database - WARNING: This will delete all data")]
migration-reset:
    cargo run --bin {{project-name}} -- migrate migration reset

[doc("Inspect the current database schema as SQL")]
migration-inspect:
    cargo run --bin {{project-name}} -- migrate migration inspect

fmt:
    cargo fmt

[doc("Check code formatting without making changes")]
fmt-check:
    cargo fmt -- --check

[doc("Run clippy linter")]
clippy:
    cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features

[doc("Run clippy with all warnings treated as errors")]
clippy-strict:
    cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings

[doc("Run all code quality checks (fmt, clippy, tests)")]
check: fmt-check clippy test

# Utility Commands

update:
    cargo update

[doc("Check for outdated dependencies (requires cargo-outdated)")]
outdated:
    cargo outdated

[doc("Generate a new dependency graph (requires cargo-graph)")]
graph:
    cargo graph | dot -Tpng > dependency-graph.png

bloat:
    cargo bloat --release

    cargo audit

    zizmor .github/workflows

// FILE: ./cargo-generate.toml
# cargo-generate template configuration.
#
# This repository is a *pure* cargo-generate template: `cargo generate --git
# <repo>` renders it into a new project. Liquid delimiters (`{{ }}`, `{% %}`)
# are NOT configurable, so the boundary below is strict:
#
#   - `exclude`: copied verbatim, never touched by Liquid. Everything with
#     syntax that collides with `{{`/`{%` lives here: Askama templates,
#     GitHub Actions (`${{ }}`), vendored JS, and docs containing Askama
#     examples.
#   - `ignore`: not copied into the generated project at all.
#   - Everything else is processed with Liquid (`{{project-name}}`,
#     `{{crate_name}}`, `{% if ... %}`).
#
# Branding is rendered at *runtime* instead: `PageContext.app_name` derives a
# display name from `CARGO_PKG_NAME`, so generated projects show their own
# name without any Liquid inside `templates/`.
#
# Follow-up (not yet done): worker, storage and swagger/utoipa remain
# unconditional subsystems — they have no Cargo features today, so there is
# nothing to conditionally render. Feature-gate them first before offering
# include/exclude options here.

[template]
cargo_generate_version = ">=0.19.0"

exclude = [
    # Askama templates collide with Liquid on both `{{ }}` and `{% %}`.
    "templates",
    # GitHub Actions `${{ }}` expressions collide with Liquid `{{ }}`.
    ".github",
    # Vendored JS bundles contain `{{ }}` sequences (e.g. alpine.js).
    "static",
    # Docs that show literal Askama syntax (`{% block %}`, `{{ ctx.* }}`).
    "docs/ADD_HTMX_FORM.md",
    "docs/ADD_NEW_PAGE.md",
    "docs/ADD_PROTECTED_ROUTE.md",
    "docs/CSRF_PROTECTION.md",
    "docs/HTMX_ASKAMA_PATTERNS.md",
    # Template-authoring docs: shows literal Liquid syntax on purpose.
    "docs/TEMPLATE.md",
]

ignore = [
    # Not part of a generated project.
    "cargo-generate.toml",
    # This repository's own README — the generated project's README lives in
    # '{{"README"}}.md' (Liquid filenames render too, so it ships as README.md).
    "README.md",
    # Template-repo CI (renders + verifies the template itself). Generated
    # projects get `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, placed by `post.rhai`.
    ".github/workflows/template-ci.yml",
    # Template-authoring documentation; irrelevant to generated projects.
    "docs/TEMPLATE.md",
    # Local-only artifacts, defensive for `cargo generate --path` renders.
    ".devin",
    "docs/local",
    "target",
    "node_modules",
    "test_uploads",
    ".env",
    "axum_kickoff.db",
]

# Post-render file shuffling that `exclude`/`ignore` cannot express.
# `post.rhai` swaps `.github/ci.yml` (the real generated-project CI, inert in
# this repository because it sits outside `workflows/`) over the inert stub
# at `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, then deletes itself. Renders therefore ship
# a clean `ci.yml` with no template-detection machinery.
[hooks]
post = ["post.rhai"]

[placeholders.database]
type = "string"
prompt = "Which database backend?"
choices = ["sqlite", "postgresql"]
default = "sqlite"

[placeholders.oauth_github]
type = "bool"
prompt = "Include GitHub OAuth sign-in?"
default = true

[placeholders.oauth_google]
type = "bool"
prompt = "Include Google OAuth sign-in?"
default = false

[placeholders.oauth_facebook]
type = "bool"
prompt = "Include Facebook OAuth sign-in?"
default = false

[placeholders.metrics]
type = "bool"
prompt = "Include the Prometheus metrics endpoint?"
default = false

[placeholders.sentry]
type = "bool"
prompt = "Include Sentry error reporting?"
default = false

[placeholders.jemalloc]
type = "bool"
prompt = "Use jemalloc as the global allocator?"
default = false

[placeholders.tailwind]
type = "bool"
prompt = "Use Tailwind CSS instead of the bundled stylesheet?"
default = false

# Without `metrics`, drop the now-unused feature-gated sources so generated
# projects ship no dead code. The `#[cfg(feature = "metrics")]` declarations
# in `lib.rs`/`middleware/mod.rs`/`tests/mod.rs`/`router.rs` are inert either
# way (the feature no longer exists when the option is off).
#
# NOTE: `ignore` is matched against source paths verbatim (no globs, before
# filename substitution), so the snapshot entry intentionally spells the
# literal `{{crate_name}}` filename.
[conditional.'!metrics']
ignore = [
    "src/metrics.rs",
    "src/middleware/metrics.rs",
    "src/tests/metrics.rs",
    "src/tests/snapshots/{{crate_name}}__tests__metrics__metrics_snapshot.snap",
]

# Ship only the migration set matching the chosen database backend.
# `migrations/` is the SQLite set; `migrations-postgres/` is the PostgreSQL
# set. `Toasty.toml` renders `migration.path` to point at the kept one.
# Tests don't depend on either — `test_db.rs` builds schema via
# `Db::push_schema` through the connected driver.
[conditional.'database == "sqlite"']
ignore = ["migrations-postgres"]

[conditional.'database == "postgresql"']
ignore = ["migrations"]

# Tailwind replaces the hand-written stylesheet: `just css` compiles
# `static/css/input.css` into `static/css/style.css`, which stays the served
# path so `templates/` needs no Liquid. When Tailwind is off, drop its source
# file; when on, drop the bundled stylesheet it regenerates.
[conditional.'!tailwind']
ignore = ["static/css/input.css"]

[conditional.'tailwind']
ignore = ["static/css/style.css"]

// FILE: ./Toasty.toml
[migration]
path = '{% if database == "postgresql" %}migrations-postgres{% else %}migrations{% endif %}'
prefix_style = "Sequential"
checksums = false
statement_breakpoints = true

// FILE: ./migrations-postgres/migrations/0000_migration.sql
CREATE TABLE "background_jobs" (
    "id" BIGINT NOT NULL GENERATED BY DEFAULT AS IDENTITY,
    "queue" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "job_type" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "data" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "retries" INTEGER NOT NULL,
    "priority" SMALLINT NOT NULL,
    "run_at" TIMESTAMPTZ(6) NOT NULL,
    "created_at" TIMESTAMPTZ(6) NOT NULL,
    "locked_until" TIMESTAMPTZ(6),
    "locked_by" TEXT,
    "failed_at" TIMESTAMPTZ(6),
    PRIMARY KEY ("id")
);
CREATE INDEX "index_background_jobs_poll" ON "background_jobs" ("queue", "failed_at", "locked_until", "run_at");
CREATE TABLE "rate_limit_buckets" (
    "bucket_key" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "action" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "bucket_id" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "tokens" INTEGER NOT NULL,
    "last_refill" TIMESTAMPTZ(6) NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY ("bucket_key")
);
CREATE TABLE "users" (
    "id" BIGINT NOT NULL GENERATED BY DEFAULT AS IDENTITY,
    "provider" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "provider_user_id" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "login" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "name" TEXT,
    "email" TEXT,
    "avatar_url" TEXT,
    "is_active" BOOLEAN NOT NULL,
    "account_lock_reason" TEXT,
    "account_lock_until" TIMESTAMPTZ(6),
    "created_at" TIMESTAMPTZ(6) NOT NULL,
    "updated_at" TIMESTAMPTZ(6) NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY ("id")
);
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX "index_users_by_provider_and_provider_user_id" ON "users" ("provider", "provider_user_id");
CREATE TABLE "api_tokens" (
    "id" BIGINT NOT NULL GENERATED BY DEFAULT AS IDENTITY,
    "user_id" BIGINT NOT NULL,
    "name" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "token" BYTEA NOT NULL,
    "created_at" TIMESTAMPTZ(6) NOT NULL,
    "last_used_at" TIMESTAMPTZ(6),
    "revoked" BOOLEAN NOT NULL,
    "resource_scopes" TEXT,
    "action_scopes" TEXT,
    "expired_at" TIMESTAMPTZ(6),
    PRIMARY KEY ("id")
);
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX "index_api_tokens_by_token" ON "api_tokens" ("token");
CREATE TABLE "posts" (
    "id" BIGINT NOT NULL GENERATED BY DEFAULT AS IDENTITY,
    "user_id" BIGINT NOT NULL,
    "title" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "content" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "published" BOOLEAN NOT NULL,
    "created_at" TIMESTAMPTZ(6) NOT NULL,
    "updated_at" TIMESTAMPTZ(6) NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY ("id")
);
CREATE INDEX "index_posts_by_user_id" ON "posts" ("user_id");

// FILE: ./post.rhai
// Replaces the inert `.github/workflows/ci.yml` stub (which exists only for
// the template repository, where the checkout cannot compile) with the real
// generated-project CI shipped verbatim at `.github/ci.yml`, so rendered
// projects get a clean workflow with no template-detection machinery.
file::delete(".github/workflows/ci.yml");
file::rename(".github/ci.yml", ".github/workflows/ci.yml");

// The hook file itself is not part of a generated project.
file::delete("post.rhai");

// FILE: ./.cargo/audit.toml
[advisories]
ignore = [
    # rsa is pulled into Cargo.lock only as an optional dependency of
    # `toasty-driver-mysql` (via `sqlx-mysql`). The mysql/mariadb driver
    # features are never enabled — the only database backends are sqlite
    # and postgresql — so the crate is never compiled. The Marvin Attack
    # advisory has no fixed release; revisit if a mysql backend is added.
    "RUSTSEC-2023-0071",
]

// FILE: ./scripts/rendered-check.sh
#!/bin/sh
# Runs a cargo check that needs a parseable Cargo.toml.
#
# In the template checkout the manifest contains Liquid placeholders, so the
# tool is run inside a freshly rendered default project in a temp dir — the
# same thing .github/workflows/template-ci.yml's deps job does. This keeps
# prek hooks honest in the template repo instead of skipping them.
#
# In a generated project cargo-generate.toml is absent and the tool runs
# directly (fmt stays a fixer there).
#
# Usage: rendered-check.sh <fmt|deny|machete>
set -eu

check="${1:?usage: rendered-check.sh <fmt|deny|machete>}"

if [ -f cargo-generate.toml ]; then
    tmp="$(mktemp -d)"
    trap 'rm -rf "$tmp"' EXIT
    cargo generate --path . --name rendered-check \
        --destination "$tmp" --vcs none --silent
    cd "$tmp/rendered-check"
    case "$check" in
        fmt) exec cargo fmt --check ;;
        deny) exec cargo deny check ;;
        machete) exec cargo machete ;;
    esac
else
    case "$check" in
        fmt) exec cargo fmt ;;
        deny) exec cargo deny check ;;
        machete) exec cargo machete ;;
    esac
fi
echo "rendered-check.sh: unknown check '$check'" >&2
exit 2

// FILE: ./prek.toml
# Pre-commit hooks for {{project-name}}
# Install prek: cargo install prek
# Run: prek install
# Skip: SKIP=cargo-fmt git commit ...
#
# Template-repo note: this file ships to generated projects. In the template
# checkout itself, `cargo-generate.toml` exists and `Cargo.toml` contains
# Liquid placeholders, so tools that need a parseable manifest can't run
# directly. fmt/deny/machete call scripts/rendered-check.sh, which renders a
# default project and runs there (like template-ci.yml's deps job); clippy/doc
# need a full build, so they no-op on the marker file instead — same
# convention as .github/workflows/ci.yml, and template-ci covers them.

fail_fast = false

# Priority 0: Read-only hooks and file type-specific fixers
[[repos]]
repo = "https://github.com/pre-commit/pre-commit-hooks"
rev = "v6.0.0"
hooks = [
  { id = "check-merge-conflict" },
  { id = "trailing-whitespace" },
  { id = "end-of-file-fixer" },
  { id = "check-yaml" },
  # Cargo.toml is not valid TOML in the template checkout (Liquid); cargo
  # itself validates the rendered manifest in generated projects.
  { id = "check-toml", exclude = "Cargo.toml" },
  { id = "check-added-large-files", args = ["--maxkb=1000"] },
]

# Spell checking
# `--isolated` stops typos from parsing Cargo.toml looking for config — in the
# template checkout that file isn't valid TOML. Drop the flag if a generated
# project adds its own `_typos.toml`.
[[repos]]
repo = "https://github.com/crate-ci/typos"
rev = "v1.47.1"
hooks = [
  { id = "typos", args = ["--isolated"] },
]

# GitHub Actions workflow validation
[[repos]]
repo = "https://github.com/python-jsonschema/check-jsonschema"
rev = "0.37.2"
hooks = [
  { id = "check-github-workflows" },
]

# GitHub Actions linting
[[repos]]
repo = "https://github.com/rhysd/actionlint"
rev = "v1.7.12"
hooks = [
  { id = "actionlint" },
]

# Rust formatting — runs `cargo fmt --check` on a rendered project in the
# template checkout; stays a fixer in generated projects.
[[repos]]
repo = "local"
[[repos.hooks]]
id = "rustfmt"
name = "rustfmt"
entry = "scripts/rendered-check.sh fmt"
language = "system"
pass_filenames = false

# Rust clippy linting — needs a full build, so it only runs in generated
# projects; template-ci.yml covers it on renders.
[[repos]]
repo = "local"
[[repos.hooks]]
id = "cargo-clippy"
name = "cargo clippy"
entry = "sh -c 'test -f cargo-generate.toml || exec cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features --manifest-path Cargo.toml'"
language = "system"
pass_filenames = false
env = { RUSTFLAGS = "-D warnings", RUSTDOCFLAGS = "-D warnings" }

# Rust documentation check — same as clippy: skipped in the template checkout.
[[repos]]
repo = "local"
[[repos.hooks]]
id = "cargo-doc"
name = "cargo doc"
entry = "sh -c 'test -f cargo-generate.toml || exec cargo doc --no-deps --document-private-items --manifest-path Cargo.toml'"
language = "system"
pass_filenames = false

# Cargo deny check (dependencies) — runs on a rendered project in the
# template checkout, so shipped Cargo.lock advisories are caught pre-push.
[[repos]]
repo = "local"
[[repos.hooks]]
id = "cargo-deny"
name = "cargo deny"
entry = "scripts/rendered-check.sh deny"
language = "system"
pass_filenames = false

# Cargo machete (unused dependencies)
[[repos]]

// FILE: ./.env.sample
# Environment Variables Sample
# Copy this file to .env and fill in the values for your local development

# Required: Application environment
# One of: development, test, production
# Secure by default: an unset APP_ENV means production, so keep this set to
# `development` for local work (enables the /debug route, non-Secure cookies,
# and pretty logs).
APP_ENV=development

# Optional: Database connection URL for tests
# TEST_DATABASE_URL=sqlite::memory:

# Required: Database connection URL
{% if database == "postgresql" %}# PostgreSQL (selected backend):
DATABASE_URL=postgresql://postgres:postgres@localhost:5432/{{crate_name}}
# For SQLite instead:
# DATABASE_URL=sqlite:./{{crate_name}}.db
# For in-memory SQLite (for testing):
# DATABASE_URL=sqlite::memory:{% else %}# For local development with SQLite (default):
# DATABASE_URL=sqlite:./{{crate_name}}.db
# For in-memory SQLite (for testing):
# DATABASE_URL=sqlite::memory:
# For PostgreSQL:
# DATABASE_URL=postgresql://user:password@localhost:5432/{{crate_name}}
DATABASE_URL=sqlite:./{{crate_name}}.db{% endif %}

# Required: Session key for signing and encrypting cookies
# Generate a secure random key. In production, this should be a long, random string.
SESSION_KEY=use-a-session-key-with-64-bytes-or-more-here-and-store-it-securely

# Optional: Force the Secure flag on session cookies (true/false/1/0)
# Defaults to true in production and false in development/test. Set this to
# override the default, e.g. to test Secure cookies in development.
# SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE=false

# OAuth sign-in providers (selected at generation time). Each provider is
# enabled when BOTH its client ID and secret are set; unset both to disable
# it, or remove a provider entirely by regenerating with the matching
# `oauth_*` option turned off. Setting only one is a startup error.
{% if oauth_github %}# GitHub OAuth — create an OAuth app at https://github.com/settings/developers
GH_CLIENT_ID=your_github_client_id
GH_CLIENT_SECRET=your_github_client_secret
# Optional: callback URL override (defaults to http://localhost:8888/... in dev, https://<domain>:<port>/... in production)
# GH_REDIRECT_URI=http://localhost:8888/api/v1/auth/github/callback
{% endif %}{% if oauth_google %}# Google OAuth — create an OAuth client at https://console.cloud.google.com/apis/credentials
# GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID=your_google_client_id
# GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET=your_google_client_secret
# GOOGLE_REDIRECT_URI=http://localhost:8888/api/v1/auth/google/callback
{% endif %}{% if oauth_facebook %}# Facebook OAuth — create an app at https://developers.facebook.com/apps (add
# the "Facebook Login" product and list the redirect URI under "Valid OAuth
# Redirect URIs")
# FACEBOOK_CLIENT_ID=your_facebook_app_id
# FACEBOOK_CLIENT_SECRET=your_facebook_app_secret
# FACEBOOK_REDIRECT_URI=http://localhost:8888/api/v1/auth/facebook/callback
{% endif %}

# Required: Comma-separated list of allowed CORS origins
# For local development, include your frontend URL(s)
WEB_ALLOWED_ORIGINS=http://localhost:8888,http://127.0.0.1:8888

# Optional: Port to listen on (defaults to 8888)
PORT=8888

# Optional: Domain name of the application (defaults to "localhost")
DOMAIN_NAME=localhost

# Optional: Trusted proxy IPs/CIDR ranges for X-Forwarded-For header
# Format: Comma-separated list in CIDR notation (e.g., "127.0.0.1/32,::1/128,10.0.0.0/8")
# Defaults to "127.0.0.1/32,::1/128" (localhost) for safety
# TRUSTED_PROXIES=127.0.0.1/32,::1/128

# Optional: Maximum number of blocking threads for `spawn_blocking` work
# (leave unset for the Tokio default of 512)
# SERVER_THREADS=512

# Optional: Number of async worker threads for the Tokio runtime
# (leave unset for the default: the number of available CPU cores)
# SERVER_CORE_THREADS=4

# Optional: Set to any value to indicate running in Docker
# DEV_DOCKER=1

# Optional: Set to any value to indicate running on Heroku
# HEROKU=1

{% if metrics %}# Optional: Prometheus metrics access token
# METRICS_TOKEN=your_metrics_token
{% endif %}
{% if sentry %}# Optional: Sentry DSN for error reporting (only active in production)
# SENTRY_DSN=https://public@o0.ingest.sentry.io/0
{% endif %}

# Optional: Logging format
# One of: pretty, full, compact, json (defaults to pretty in dev/test, json in production)
# LOG_FORMAT=pretty

# Storage Configuration
# Optional: Path for local filesystem storage (defaults to "./local_uploads")
# STORAGE_PATH=./local_uploads

# Optional: CDN prefix for generating public URLs (e.g., "cdn.example.com")
# CDN_PREFIX=

# Security: Rate Limiting Configuration
# Format: RATE_LIMITER_{ACTION}_RATE_SECONDS and RATE_LIMITER_{ACTION}_BURST
# Actions: API_REQUEST, LOGIN_ATTEMPT, PASSWORD_RESET, FILE_UPLOAD, FORM_SUBMISSION, OAUTH_AUTHORIZE, OAUTH_CALLBACK, TOKEN_CREATION, PUBLIC_API_READ
# RATE_LIMITER_API_REQUEST_RATE_SECONDS=1
# RATE_LIMITER_API_REQUEST_BURST=10
# RATE_LIMITER_LOGIN_ATTEMPT_RATE_SECONDS=5
# RATE_LIMITER_LOGIN_ATTEMPT_BURST=5
# RATE_LIMITER_PASSWORD_RESET_RATE_SECONDS=60
# RATE_LIMITER_PASSWORD_RESET_BURST=3
# RATE_LIMITER_FILE_UPLOAD_RATE_SECONDS=10
# RATE_LIMITER_FILE_UPLOAD_BURST=5
# RATE_LIMITER_FORM_SUBMISSION_RATE_SECONDS=30
# RATE_LIMITER_FORM_SUBMISSION_BURST=10
# RATE_LIMITER_OAUTH_AUTHORIZE_RATE_SECONDS=5
# RATE_LIMITER_OAUTH_AUTHORIZE_BURST=5
# RATE_LIMITER_OAUTH_CALLBACK_RATE_SECONDS=5
# RATE_LIMITER_OAUTH_CALLBACK_BURST=5
# RATE_LIMITER_TOKEN_CREATION_RATE_SECONDS=10
# RATE_LIMITER_TOKEN_CREATION_BURST=3
# RATE_LIMITER_PUBLIC_API_READ_RATE_SECONDS=1
# RATE_LIMITER_PUBLIC_API_READ_BURST=60

# Security: Blocked IP addresses (comma-separated)
# BLOCKED_IPS=192.168.1.100,10.0.0.50

# Security: Blocked route patterns (comma-separated)
# Matched against axum route templates (MatchedPath), not literal request
# paths — e.g. use /api/v1/posts/{id} to block every post id.
# BLOCKED_ROUTES=/api/admin,/api/internal

# Security: Blocked traffic by headers
# Format: HeaderName=ENV_VAR_NAME,HeaderName2=ENV_VAR_NAME2
# Each ENV_VAR should contain comma-separated values to block
# Values starting and ending with / are treated as regex patterns
# BLOCKED_TRAFFIC=User-Agent=BLOCKED_UAS
# BLOCKED_UAS=/curl\/[\d]+\.[\d]+\.[\d]+/,bad-bot

# Security: Security Headers Configuration
# Enable HSTS (HTTPS only - requires HTTPS to be effective)
# SECURITY_HSTS_ENABLED=false

# HSTS max-age in seconds (default: 31536000 = 1 year)
# SECURITY_HSTS_MAX_AGE=31536000

# HSTS includeSubDomains directive
# SECURITY_HSTS_INCLUDE_SUBDOMAINS=true

# HSTS preload directive (for inclusion in browser preload lists)
# SECURITY_HSTS_PRELOAD=false

# Content-Security-Policy mode: strict, permissive, or custom:CSP_STRING
# SECURITY_CSP_MODE=strict

# X-Frame-Options: deny, sameorigin, or allow-from:URL
# SECURITY_FRAME_OPTIONS=deny

# Custom frame ancestors for CSP (overrides frame-ancestors directive)
# SECURITY_FRAME_ANCESTORS=https://trusted-domain.com

# Referrer-Policy: no-referrer, no-referrer-when-downgrade, unsafe-url,
# strict-origin-when-cross-origin, same-origin, strict-origin, origin-when-cross-origin
# SECURITY_REFERRER_POLICY=strict-origin-when-cross-origin

# Permissions-Policy: restrictive, permissive, or custom:POLICY_STRING
# SECURITY_PERMISSIONS_POLICY=restrictive

-- #[toasty::breakpoint]
CREATE INDEX "index_posts_by_user_id" ON "posts" ("user_id");

// FILE: ./migrations/migrations/0002_migration.sql
ALTER TABLE "background_jobs" ADD COLUMN "locked_by" TEXT;
-- #[toasty::breakpoint]
ALTER TABLE "background_jobs" ADD COLUMN "failed_at" TEXT;
-- #[toasty::breakpoint]
ALTER TABLE "background_jobs" ADD COLUMN "locked_until" TEXT;
-- #[toasty::breakpoint]
CREATE INDEX "index_background_jobs_poll" ON "background_jobs" ("queue", "failed_at", "locked_until", "run_at");
-- #[toasty::breakpoint]
-- Repair rows stranded by the old claim scheme, which marked claimed jobs by
-- setting run_at to the far-future sentinel ('9999-...'). After this, they are
-- unclaimed (locked_until IS NULL) and due (run_at <= now), so the worker
-- polls pick them up again.
UPDATE "background_jobs"
SET "run_at" = "created_at"
WHERE "run_at" > '2999-01-01';

// FILE: ./migrations/migrations/0000_migration.sql
CREATE TABLE "posts" (
    "id" INTEGER NOT NULL PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    "user_id" INTEGER NOT NULL,
    "title" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "content" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "published" BOOLEAN NOT NULL,
    "created_at" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "updated_at" TEXT NOT NULL
);
-- #[toasty::breakpoint]
CREATE TABLE "api_tokens" (
    "id" INTEGER NOT NULL PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    "user_id" INTEGER NOT NULL,
    "name" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "token" BLOB NOT NULL,
    "created_at" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "last_used_at" TEXT,
    "revoked" BOOLEAN NOT NULL,
    "resource_scopes" TEXT,
    "action_scopes" TEXT,
    "expired_at" TEXT
);
-- #[toasty::breakpoint]
CREATE TABLE "rate_limit_buckets" (
    "bucket_key" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "action" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "bucket_id" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "tokens" INTEGER NOT NULL,
    "last_refill" TEXT NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY ("bucket_key")
);
-- #[toasty::breakpoint]
CREATE TABLE "users" (
    "id" INTEGER NOT NULL PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    "provider" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "provider_user_id" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "login" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "name" TEXT,
    "email" TEXT,
    "avatar_url" TEXT,
    "is_active" BOOLEAN NOT NULL,
    "account_lock_reason" TEXT,
    "account_lock_until" TEXT,
    "created_at" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "updated_at" TEXT NOT NULL
);
-- #[toasty::breakpoint]
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX "index_users_by_provider_and_provider_user_id" ON "users" ("provider", "provider_user_id");

// FILE: ./migrations/migrations/0001_migration.sql
CREATE TABLE "background_jobs" (
    "id" INTEGER NOT NULL PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    "queue" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "job_type" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "data" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "retries" INTEGER NOT NULL,
    "priority" SMALLINT NOT NULL,
    "run_at" TEXT NOT NULL,
    "created_at" TEXT NOT NULL
);

  <meta charset="utf-8">

  <meta name="csrf-token" content="{{ ctx.csrf_token }}">
  <title>{% block title %}{% endblock %}</title>
  <link nonce="{{ ctx.csp_nonce }}" rel="stylesheet" href="/static/css/style.css">
  <script nonce="{{ ctx.csp_nonce }}" src="/static/vendor/htmx.min.js"></script>
  <script nonce="{{ ctx.csp_nonce }}" defer src="/static/vendor/alpine.js"></script>
</head>
{# `X-CSRF-Token` is a non-safelisted header — even an empty value forces a CORS
   preflight on cross-origin HTMX requests, so omit the attribute entirely when
   no token exists (anonymous sessionless pages). #}
<body{% if !ctx.csrf_token.is_empty() %} hx-headers='{"X-CSRF-Token": "{{ ctx.csrf_token | safe }}"}'{% endif %}>
{% block content %}{% endblock %}
</body>
</html>

// FILE: ./templates/examples/counter.html
{% extends "base.html" %}

{% block title %}Counter - HTMX + Askama Example{% endblock %}

{% block content %}
    <a href="#main-content" class="skip-link">Skip to main content</a>
    <nav class="navbar" aria-label="Main navigation">
        <div class="nav-container">
            <div class="nav-brand">
                <span class="logo">🔢</span>
                <span>HTMX + Askama Examples</span>
            </div>
            <div class="nav-links">
                <a href="/examples/contact">Contact Form</a>
                <a href="/">Back to Home</a>
            </div>
        </div>
    </nav>

    <div class="container" id="main-content">
        <section class="section">
            <h1 class="section-title">Counter Example</h1>
            <p class="section-text">
                This demonstrates simple state updates using HTMX partials.
                The counter value is updated without page reloads.
            </p>
        </section>

        <section class="section">
            <h2 class="section-title">Counter</h2>

            <div class="counter-container">
                <!-- Counter display - updated via HTMX -->
                <div
                    id="counter-display"
                    hx-post="/examples/counter/increment"
                    hx-trigger="load"
                    hx-swap="innerHTML"
                >
                    Loading...
                </div>

                <div class="counter-controls">
                    <!-- Decrement button -->
                    <button
                        class="btn btn-secondary-solid"
                        aria-label="Decrement counter"
                        hx-post="/examples/counter/decrement"
                        hx-target="#counter-display"
                        hx-swap="innerHTML"
                        hx-include="#counter-display"
                    >
                        - Decrement
                    </button>

                    <!-- Increment button -->
                    <button
                        class="btn btn-primary-solid"
                        aria-label="Increment counter"
                        hx-post="/examples/counter/increment"
                        hx-target="#counter-display"
                        hx-swap="innerHTML"
                        hx-include="#counter-display"
                    >
                        + Increment
                    </button>
                </div>
            </div>
        </section>

        <section class="section">
            <h2 class="section-title">How It Works</h2>
            <div class="code-block">
                <pre><code>1. Page loads: HTMX triggers POST to /examples/counter/increment
2. Server returns counter partial HTML with current count
3. User clicks increment/decrement
4. HTMX sends POST with current count value
5. Server calculates new count and returns updated partial
6. HTMX swaps the new partial into #counter-display</code></pre>
            </div>
        </section>

        <section class="section">
            <h2 class="section-title">Key HTMX Attributes Used</h2>
            <ul class="features-list">
                <li class="feature-item">
                    <span class="feature-icon">→</span>
                    <span><strong>hx-trigger="load"</strong>: Triggers request when element loads</span>
                </li>
                <li class="feature-item">
                    <span class="feature-icon">→</span>
                    <span><strong>hx-include</strong>: Includes additional form data in request</span>
                </li>
                <li class="feature-item">
                    <span class="feature-icon">→</span>
                    <span><strong>hx-swap="innerHTML"</strong>: Replaces content inside target</span>
                </li>
            </ul>
        </section>
    </div>
{% endblock %}

// FILE: ./templates/examples/contact.html
{% extends "base.html" %}

{% block title %}Contact Form - HTMX + Askama Example{% endblock %}

{% block content %}
    <a href="#main-content" class="skip-link">Skip to main content</a>
    <nav class="navbar" aria-label="Main navigation">
        <div class="nav-container">
            <div class="nav-brand">
                <span class="logo">📝</span>
                <span>HTMX + Askama Examples</span>
            </div>
            <div class="nav-links">
                <a href="/">Back to Home</a>
            </div>
        </div>
    </nav>

    <div class="container" id="main-content">
        <section class="section">
            <h1 class="section-title">Contact Form Example</h1>
            <p class="section-text">
                This demonstrates a full-page Askama template with an HTMX form that validates
                and returns partial HTML on success or error.
            </p>
        </section>

        <section class="section">
            <h2 class="section-title">Try It</h2>

            <!--
                Key HTMX attributes:
                - hx-post: HTTP method and endpoint
                - hx-target: Where to insert the response
                - hx-swap: How to insert the response (innerHTML, outerHTML, beforeend, etc.)
                - hx-indicator: Element to show during loading
            -->
            <form
                hx-post="/examples/contact"
                hx-target="#form-result"
                hx-swap="innerHTML"
                hx-indicator="#loading-indicator"
                class="contact-form"
            >
                <input type="hidden" name="csrf_token" value="{{ ctx.csrf_token }}">

                <div class="form-group">
                    <label for="name">Name *</label>
                    <input
                        type="text"
                        id="name"
                        name="name"
                        required
                        placeholder="Your name"
                        class="form-input"
                        aria-describedby="name-error"
                    >
                </div>

                <div class="form-group">
                    <label for="email">Email *</label>
                    <input
                        type="email"
                        id="email"
                        name="email"
                        required
                        placeholder="your@email.com"
                        class="form-input"
                        aria-describedby="email-error"
                    >
                </div>

                <div class="form-group">
                    <label for="message">Message *</label>
                    <textarea
                        id="message"
                        name="message"
                        required
                        rows="5"
                        placeholder="Your message..."
                        class="form-input"
                        aria-describedby="message-error"
                    ></textarea>
                </div>

                <button type="submit" class="btn btn-primary-solid">
                    Send Message
                </button>

                <div id="loading-indicator" class="loading-indicator">
                    Sending...
                </div>
            </form>

            <!-- Result container for HTMX response -->
            <div id="form-result" class="form-result" aria-live="assertive"></div>
        </section>

        <section class="section">
            <h2 class="section-title">How It Works</h2>
            <div class="code-block">
                <pre><code>1. User fills out the form and clicks "Send Message"
2. HTMX sends a POST request to /examples/contact
3. Server validates the input
4. On success: Returns a success partial template
5. On error: Returns an error partial template with validation messages
6. HTMX swaps the response into #form-result</code></pre>
            </div>
        </section>

        <section class="section">
            <h2 class="section-title">Key Patterns</h2>
            <ul class="features-list">
                <li class="feature-item">
                    <span class="feature-icon">→</span>
                    <span><strong>Full-page template</strong>: This HTML file is a complete page</span>
                </li>
                <li class="feature-item">
                    <span class="feature-icon">→</span>
                    <span><strong>HTMX form</strong>: Form submits via AJAX without page reload</span>
                </li>
                <li class="feature-item">
                    <span class="feature-icon">→</span>
                    <span><strong>Partial responses</strong>: Server returns HTML fragments, not full pages</span>
                </li>
                <li class="feature-item">
                    <span class="feature-icon">→</span>
                    <span><strong>CSRF protection</strong>: Token included via hx-headers and form field</span>
                </li>
                <li class="feature-item">
                    <span class="feature-icon">→</span>
                    <span><strong>Loading indicator</strong>: Shows during request processing</span>
                </li>
            </ul>
        </section>
    </div>
{% endblock %}

// FILE: ./templates/examples/contact_success.html
<!-- Partial template: Contact form success message -->
<!-- This is returned as HTML fragment when form submission succeeds -->

<div class="alert alert-success" role="status">
    <div class="alert-icon">✓</div>
    <div class="alert-content">
        <h4>Message Sent!</h4>
        <p>Thank you, {{ name }}. We've received your message and will get back to you at {{ email }}.</p>
        <button class="btn btn-secondary alert-clear-btn">
            Clear
        </button>
    </div>
</div>
<script nonce="{{ ctx.csp_nonce }}">
    (function() {
        document.querySelectorAll('.alert-clear-btn').forEach(function(btn) {
            btn.addEventListener('click', function() {
                var alert = btn.closest('.alert');
                if (alert) alert.remove();
            });
        });
    })();
</script>
